    best=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)

Let's define FFT size helper:
```
def next_power_of_two(x):
    n=1
    while n < x:
        n *=2
    return n
```

`fft_convolve_full(p)`:
```
def fft_convolve_full(p):
    n=p.size
    N=1
    size=2*n-1
    while N<size: N*=2
    fp=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    q=np.fft.irfft(fp*fp,N)
    return q[:size]
```
This product `fp*fp` works for linear convolution (not autocorr). For real p, result should be real. Use `np.real` just in case.

`fast_score(p)`:
```
def fast_score(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p=np.clip(p,0.0,1000.0)
    s=float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<0.01: return float('inf')
    n=p.size
    if n==0:return inf
    q=fft_convolve_full(p)
    mb=float(q.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mb): return inf
    return float(2.0*n*mb/(s*s))
```
For normalized sum=1, sum no issue.

`central_sequence(n, normalize=False)`:
```
def central_sequence(n, normalize=True):
    p=np.empty(n); p[0]=1.0
    for i in range(1,n): p[i]=p[i-1]*(2*i-1)/(2*i)
    if normalize: p/=p.sum()
    return p
```
For n large 16384, loop ok. Could vectorize cumulative product of ratios using logs for speed but loop 16k negligible. For n=100000 loop ok too.

def normalize_nonnegative(p):

    p=np.where(np.isfinite(p), p, 0.0)
    p=np.clip(p,0,1000)
    s=p.sum()

    if s>=0.01 and s<=1000? For optimization, scale to sum1: p/=s

Projection/clip normalization:
```
def clip_normalize(p):
    p=np.asarray(p,float).copy()
    p[p<0]=0
    s=p.sum()
    if s<=0: return None
    return p/s
```
If values >1000 after normalize? p sum1 so values ≤1, fine.

`top_k_indices(q, K)`:
```
def top_k_indices(q,K):
    L=q.size
    K=min(K,L)
    if K>=L: return np.arange(L)
    part=np.argpartition(q, L-K)[L-K:]
    return part
```
Use if K maybe 64.

Optimize loop:
```
def gradient_optimize(p0, deadline, rng, K=128, max_iter=None):
    p=normalize(p0)
    best=p.copy(); best_score=fast_score(p)
    eta_base=1.0/(p.size * max(1e-12, ...))
    stall=0; sleep not.
    while time.monotonic()<deadline-2 and max_iter...:
        q=fft_convolve_full(p)
        m=q.max()
        active=top_k_indices(q,K)
        # maybe include a few near-max random to break symmetry
        vals=q[active]; mval=vals.max()
        # active weights with softmax temperature based on max variation
        temp = max(1e-12, 0.1*(mval - vals.min() + 1e-12))
        wv=np.exp((vals-mval)/temp); wv/=wv.sum()
        v=np.zeros(n)
        for idx,wi in zip(active,wv):
            if wi<=0: continue
            start=max(0, idx-(n-1)); stop=min(n, idx+1)
            if stop>start:
                first=start; last=stop-1
                v[first:stop] += wi * p[idx-first : idx-last+1][::-1]
        v*=2.0
        vm=float(np.dot(p,v))
        direction=v-vm
        # step size: scale to 5% of p median?
        maxdir=float(np.max(np.abs(direction)))
        if maxdir<=0: break
        # try multiplicities? start step = 0.5/(n*maxdir)?? Let's use step = 0.1/(p.size*maxdir)
        step = 0.1/(p.size*maxdir) # for n=4096,maxdir~0.03 =>0.006
        # line search powers: [step, 2*step,4*step?], but if too large fail.
        best_new=None; best_new_score=inf
        for mult in [0.1,0.2,0.5,1.0,2.0,4.0]:
            eta=step*mult
            pnew=p - eta*direction
            pnew=clip_normalize(pnew)
            if pnew is None: continue
            sc=fast_score(pnew)
            if sc<best_new_score:
                best_new_score=sc; best_new=pnew
        if best_new is not None and best_new_score < best_score*(1-1e-12):
            p=best_new; best_score=...
            stall=0
        else:
            # accept small random perturbation to escape
            if rng.random()<0.5:
                noise=rng.normal(0,0.05,p) # on p? or log?
                pnew=clip_normalize(p + noise*p)
                if pnew is not None:
                   sc=fast_score(pnew)
                   if sc < best_score*1.01: p=pnew; best_score=min(...)
            stall +=1
            if stall>100: # reinitialize with small random from central? maybe break
                break
```

```
_CUR_N = 0
_CUR_T = 1.0
_CUR_P = None # not needed
def _theta_fun_grad(theta):
    n=_CUR_N; T=_CUR_T
    # stable softmax
    th = theta - np.max(theta)
    exp = np.exp(th); p=exp/exp.sum()
    q=fft_convolve_full(p); m=q.max()
    val = m + T * np.log(np.sum(np.exp((q-m)/T))/q.size)
    # weights
    u=(q-m)/T
    # To avoid all underflow? u max0; if T tiny, many zeros, sum maybe 1. Use stable.
    wv=np.exp(u)
    s=wv.sum()
    if s<=0: wv=top_k_uniform???
    else: wv/=s
    P=np.fft.rfft(p, N) maybe N=next_power(3n-2)
    W=np.fft.rfft(wv, N)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(W)*P,N)[:n]
    v=2*corr
    grad = p*(v - np.dot(p,v))
    return val, grad
```
But for `rfft` of `wv` length L, we need zero pad to N. `np.fft.rfft(wv, N)` works. `P` maybe also used for q? We can compute q via P with same N: q_full=irfft(P*P,N)[:2n-1]. Good. Use one P. N must >=L+n-1 for corr, and >=2n-1 for q; 3n-2 suffices. For n=2048, N=8192.

Potential issue: Softmax parameterization has scale invariance; objective F invariant to adding constant to theta, gradient has zero sum? grad sum=∑p(v-m)=0. The Hessian has flat direction; BFGS/L-BFGS may struggle. We can fix one parameter, e.g., set last theta=0 to remove scale. But softmax with BFGS can handle if we use `L-BFGS-B`? Function `minimize_sequence(theta?)`? Since we use x. Define:
```
def _minimize_seq_x(x):
    n=_OPT_N; T=_OPT_T
    # build theta full length n
    theta = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    theta[0]=0.0
    theta[1:]=x
    # clip x for stability? Use stable softmax with x clipped to [-700,700]
    th = theta
    # If max th > 700? We'll subtract max
    mx = th.max()
    if not np.isfinite(mx): mx=0
    th = th - mx
    # For extreme negative, exp underflows to 0, okay
    e = np.exp(th)
    s=e.sum()
    if s<=0 or not np.isfinite(s):
        # fallback uniform? return large
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n-1)
    p=e/s
    N=1; size_corr=3*n-2
    while N<size_corr: N*=2
    P=np.fft.rfft(p,N)
    q=np.fft.irfft(P*P,N)
    size_q=2*n-1
    q=q[:size_q]
    # due to roundoff, q might be tiny negative; clip for weights? Conv coefficients nonnegative theoretically. For score, use max(max,0)
    m=float(q.max()) if q.size else 0.0
    if m<0: m=0
    # value: m + T logsumexp((q-m)/T)/L
    # For T very small, weights may concentrate. Use stable:
    if T <= 1e-12:
        # active top? Return exact max value, gradient top uniform
        K=min(8,size_q)
        active=np.argpartition(q, size_q-K)[-K:]
        w=np.zeros(size_q); w[active]=1/K
    else:
        u=(q-m)/T
        # clip u lower -745
        u=np.clip(u, -745.0, 0.0)
        e2=np.exp(u)
        s2=e2.sum()
        if s2<=0 or not np.isfinite(s2):
            active=np.arange(size_q); w=np.full(size_q,1/size_q)
        else:
            w=e2/s2
    P2=P # already
    W=np.fft.rfft(w,N)
    corr=np.fft.irfft(np.conj(W)*P,N)
    corr=corr[:n]
    v=2.0*corr
    # numerical v may have tiny negatives; okay
    mv=float(np.dot(p,v))
    grad_full = p*(v-mv)
    grad = grad_full[1:]
    # clean nonfinite
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(grad)):
        grad = np.nan_to_num(grad, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)
    val = m + T * float(np.log(float(np.sum(e2)/size_q) if T>1e-12 else 1.0)) if T>1e-12 else m
    # To prevent BFGS confusion, add small constant? no
    return float(val), grad
```
But if T small and we fallback top K, value m; gradient uses top weights. For BFGS, objective discontinuous? T not zero. We'll use T >0.

The objective value using LSE: `m + T log(sum(e2)/L)` where e2=exp((q-m)/T), sum(e2) includes one ≈1. If T small, sum/L ~1/L, value ≈m - T log L, which is less than m by T log L. This offset constant for fixed T; gradient unaffected except weights. But BFGS minimizes value; constant okay. If T changes between runs, not comparable. Use T fixed per minimize.

When T=1e-3 and m~1e-4, T may be >m; LSE too smooth. Choose T relative to m: e.g. `T = frac * m` with frac in [0.05,0.2,0.5]. If m~1/n, T small. For n=4096, T~2e-5 to1e-4. In objective, `(q-m)/T` values O(1). Good. If T too small, numeric. Use max(T, 1e-8).

Variant function top-level:
```
def make_variant_starts(base, rng, max_candidates=30):
    n=base.size
    t=np.arange(n,dtype=float)/(max(1,n-1))
    outs=[]
    outs.append(base.copy())
    for a in (-1.0,-0.5,0.0,0.5,1.0):
        p=base*np.exp(a*t); outs.append(p)
    for a in (-1.0,1.0):
        p=base*(1.0 + a*0.3*t); # positive
        if p.min()<0: p=np.clip(p,1e-300,None)
        outs.append(p)
    for k in (1,2,3):
        p=base*np.exp(0.3*np.sin(2*np.pi*k*t)); outs.append(p)
    for _ in range(5):
        sigma=rng.normal(0,0.1, n)
        p=base*np.exp(0.2*sigma); outs.append(p)
    for p in outs: p=normalize_simplex(p); if p not None append
    return outs
```

def custom_gradient_refine(p, deadline, rng, K=96, max_iter=5000):
    p=normalize_simplex(p)
    best=p.copy(); best_sc=fast_score(p)
    q=fft_convolve_full(p)
    eta=1.0/(p.size * max(1e-12, 2.0/K)) # ~K/(2n)
    iter=0
    while time.monotonic()<deadline and iter<max_iter:
       if q is None or iter>0: q=fft_convolve_full(p)
       m=q.max(); active=top_k_indices(q,K)
       # weights: use all in active equal, but maybe random subset if equal
       v=zeros; for idx in active: ... 1/K
       v*=2
       direction=v - np.dot(p,v)
       maxd=np.max(np.abs(direction))
       if maxd<=0: break
       step=1.0/(p.size*maxd)  # change ~1?
       # try small steps: [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1]*step? This changes ~0.1-1 p.
       best_new=None
       for f in [0.01,0.02,0.05,0.1]:
           pnew=p - f*step*direction
           pnew=clip_normalize(pnew)
           if pnew is None: continue
           sc=fast_score(pnew)
           if sc<best_new_sc: ...
       if accept: update q=None (new q needed)
       else:
           # try random local noise
           if rng.random()<0.3:
              pnew=clip_normalize(p + rng.normal(0,0.01,p)*p)
              sc=fast_score(pnew); if sc<best_sc*1.001: update
           if consecutive failures>50: break or restart with small geometric noise
       iter++
```

But if p values vary, relative change for large p can be more; line search will handle.

Implement `direct_gradient_from_active(p, active, weight=None)`:
```
def direct_gradient_from_active(p, active, weights=None):
    n=p.size
    v=np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if weights is None:
        weights=np.full(len(active), 1.0/len(active))
    for idx, wi in zip(active, weights):
        if wi==0: continue
        start = idx - (n-1)
        if start < 0: start = 0
        stop = idx + 1
        if stop > n: stop = n
        if stop <= start: continue
        first=start
        last=stop-1
        # segment p[idx-first : idx-last+1]
        seg = p[idx-first : idx-last+1]
        v[first:stop] += wi * seg[::-1]
    v *= 2.0
    return v
```
Check segment bounds: idx up to 2n-2. start=max(0, idx-n+1). stop=min(n,idx+1). first=start. idx-first >=0 because start<=idx. idx-last+1 = idx-(stop-1)+1. Since stop≤idx+1, ≥0. Also idx-first <n? start=max ensures idx-first ≤ n-1. seg length = (idx-first) - (idx-last+1)+1 = last-first = stop-start. good.

If active count small, loop fine. If idx and start large, seg sizes. Good.

`custom_refine` code:
```
def custom_refine(p, deadline, rng, K=48, max_iter=20000):
    p=normalize_simplex(p)
    if p is None: return None, inf
    best=p.copy(); best_sc=fast_score(p)
    n=p.size
    fail=0
    iter=0
    # adaptive K maybe increase if stuck
    K_use=K
    while time.monotonic()<deadline and iter<max_iter:
        q=fft_convolve_full(p)
        m=q.max()
        if not finite: break
        active=top_k_indices(q,K_use)
        # if many ties, randomize subset: if m - q[active].min() < 1e-10, maybe keep
        v=direct_gradient_from_active(p, active, None)
        direction=v - np.dot(p,v)
        maxd=float(np.max(np.abs(direction)))
        if maxd<=1e-30:
            fail+=10
        else:
            step=10.0/(n*maxd)
            best_new=None; best_new_sc=best_sc
            for f in (0.02,0.05,0.1,0.3):
                pnew=p - f*step*direction
                pnew=clip_normalize(pnew)
                if pnew is None: continue
                sc=fast_score(pnew)
                if sc < best_new_sc:
                    best_new_sc=sc; best_new=pnew
            if best_new is not None and best_new_sc < best_sc:
                p=best_new; best_sc=best_new_sc; best=p.copy()
                fail=0
            else:
                fail+=1
                # occasional random acceptance
                if fail % 20 == 0:
                    pnew=clip_normalize(p + 0.05*rng.normal(0.0,1.0,n)*p)
                    if pnew is not None:
                        sc=fast_score(pnew)
                        if sc < best_sc*1.01:
                            p=pnew
                            if sc<best_sc: best_sc=sc; best=pnew.copy()
        # if stall long, random restart from central * exp noise
        if fail > 100:
            base=central_sequence(n, normalize=True)
            t=np.arange(n,dtype=float)/(n-1)
            alpha=float(rng.normal(0,0.5)?) Wait normal returns float; alpha = float(rng.normal(0.0,0.5))
            p=normalize_simplex(base*np.exp(alpha*t))
            sc=fast_score(p)
            p=p; fail=0
            iter=0? max_iter reset? not.
        iter += 1
    return best,best_sc
```
The `rng.normal(0,0.5)` returns np.float64; in code `alpha = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.5))`. For multiple random, okay.

`clip_normalize` top-level:
```
def clip_normalize(a):
    a=np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a<0]=0.0
    s=float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s<=0: return None
    a/=s
    return a
```
`normalize_simplex` similar but can scale if values >0.

`make_variants`:
```
def make_variants(base, rng, max_count=24):
    n=base.size; t=np.arange(n,dtype=float)/max(1,n-1)
    outs=[]
    outs.append(base.copy())
    for a in (-1.5,-1,-0.5,-0.25,0.25,0.5,1,1.5):
        p=base*np.exp(a*t)
        outs.append(p)
    for a in (-0.5,0.5):
        p=base*(1.0 + 0.5*a*t)
        outs.append(p)
    for k in (1,2,3):
        p=base*np.exp(0.25*np.sin(2*np.pi*k*t))
        outs.append(p)
    for _ in range(6):
        sigma=rng.normal(0.0,0.1,n)
        p=base*np.exp(0.2*sigma)
        outs.append(p)
    # normalize
    res=[]
    for p in outs:
       pn=normalize_simplex(p)
       if pn is not None: res.append(pn)
    return res
```
No nested.

`run_scipy` top-level maybe:
```
def run_scipy(candidate, n, T, maxiter):
    global _OPT_N, _OPT_T, _OPT_NFFT
    _OPT_N=n; _OPT_T=T
    size=3*n-2; N=1; while N<size: N*=2; _OPT_NFFT=N
    x=np.log(np.maximum(candidate[1:],1e-300)) - np.log(max(candidate[0],1e-300))
    x=np.clip(x, -30.0, 30.0)
    try:
       res=scipy_minimize(_seq_obj_grad, x, jac=True, method='L-BFGS-B', options={...})
       if res.x is not None:
           p=x_to_p(res.x,n)
           return p
    except Exception:
       return None
    return None
```
Need global declaration in function if assigning globals: `global _OPT_N, _OPT_T, _OPT_NFFT`.
Import: `try: from scipy.optimize import minimize as scipy_minimize except: scipy_minimize=None`.

`x_to_p`:
```
def x_to_p(x,n):
    theta=np.empty(n); theta[0]=0; theta[1:]=x
    mx=theta.max(); if not finite mx=0; theta-=mx
    e=np.exp(theta); s=e.sum(); if s<=0: return None; return e/s
```

diff = q - m
diff = np.minimum(diff, 0.0)
u = diff / T
u = np.clip(u, -745.0, 0.0)
```

```python
"""
Search for non-negative step-function heights minimizing

    2 * len(a) * max(convolve(a, a)) / (sum(a))**2

using three complementary ideas:

1. Central-binomial (truncated (1-x)^(-1/2)) sequences, which are excellent
   structured starting points and give a strong baseline near pi/2.
2. Smoothed log-sum-exp projected-gradient / L-BFGS refinement on the
   normalized simplex, using FFT-based convolutions and gradients.
3. Light basin-hopping / projected gradient polishing from several variants
   (geometric, cosine, smooth random perturbations).

The returned sequence is scaled to sum 100, keeping it safely inside the
evaluator's [0, 1000] clipping range.
"""

import numpy as np
import time
import math

try:
    from scipy.optimize import minimize as scipy_minimize
except Exception:
    scipy_minimize = None


# Globals used by the scipy objective, to avoid closures/unnecessary arguments.
_OPT_N = 0
_OPT_T = 1.0
_OPT_NFFT = 1


def next_pow2(x):
    n = 1
    while n < x:
        n <<= 1
    return n


def normalize_simplex(a):
    """Clip to non-negative finite values and scale to sum 1."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    a /= s
    return a


def clip_normalize(a):
    """Fast non-negative normalization used by projected gradient steps."""
    a = np.asarray(a, dtype=np.float64).ravel().copy()
    a[~np.isfinite(a)] = 0.0
    a[a < 0.0] = 0.0
    s = float(a.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    a /= s
    return a


def central_sequence(n, normalize=True):
    """
    Coefficients of the truncated series (1-x)^(-1/2):
    a_0 = 1, a_k = a_{k-1} * (2k-1)/(2k).
    """
    if n <= 0:
        return np.empty(0, dtype=np.float64)
    p = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    p[0] = 1.0
    for k in range(1, n):
        p[k] = p[k - 1] * ((2 * k - 1) / (2 * k))
    if normalize:
        s = float(p.sum())
        if s > 0.0:
            p /= s
    return p


def fft_convolve_full(p):
    """Full linear convolution via FFT: conv(p, p)."""
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    n = p.size
    size = 2 * n - 1
    N = next_pow2(size)
    fp = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(fp * fp, N)
    return q[:size]


def fast_score(p):
    """
    Fast local evaluation, equivalent to evaluate_sequence for valid inputs,
    but FFT-based and safe for many local iterations.
    """
    p = np.asarray(p, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
    p = np.clip(p, 0.0, 1000.0)
    n = p.size
    if n == 0:
        return float("inf")
    s = float(p.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s < 0.01:
        return float("inf")
    q = fft_convolve_full(p)
    mb = float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(mb):
        return float("inf")
    if mb < 0.0:
        mb = 0.0
    return float(2.0 * n * mb / (s * s))


def consider_candidate(best_score, best_p, p):
    """Normalize p and update the best if it improves."""
    pn = normalize_simplex(p)
    if pn is None:
        return best_score, best_p
    sc = fast_score(pn)
    if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_score:
        return sc, pn
    return best_score, best_p


def top_k_indices(q, k):
    """Indices of the k largest values of q."""
    size = q.size
    k = int(min(max(1, k), size))
    if k >= size:
        return np.arange(size)
    return np.argpartition(q, size - k)[size - k:]


def direct_gradient_from_active(p, active):
    """
    Gradient of sum_k w_k q_k for w uniform over active indices,
    q = p*p, returned without the 2 factor convention:
    v_j = 2 * sum_k w_k p_{k-j} (valid).
    """
    n = p.size
    v = np.zeros(n, dtype=np.float64)
    if active.size == 0:
        return v
    w = 1.0 / float(active.size)
    for idx in active:
        idx = int(idx)
        start = idx - (n - 1)
        if start < 0:
            start = 0
        stop = idx + 1
        if stop > n:
            stop = n
        if stop <= start:
            continue
        first = start
        last = stop - 1
        # For this fixed k=idx, contribution is w*p_{idx-j} for j=first..last.
        seg = p[idx - first : idx - last + 1]
        v[first:stop] += w * seg[::-1]
    v *= 2.0
    return v


def custom_refine(p, deadline, rng, k_active=48, max_iter=50000):
    """
    Robust projected-gradient / basin-hopping refinement on the simplex.
    """
    p = normalize_simplex(p)
    if p is None:
        return None, float("inf")
    best_p = p.copy()
    best_sc = fast_score(best_p)
    n = best_p.size
    fail = 0
    it = 0

    while time.monotonic() < deadline and it < max_iter:
        q = fft_convolve_full(best_p)
        if q.size == 0:
            break
        m = float(np.max(q))
        if not np.isfinite(m):
            break

        active = top_k_indices(q, k_active)
        v = direct_gradient_from_active(best_p, active)
        if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
            break

        mean_v = float(np.dot(best_p, v))
        direction = v - mean_v
        maxd = float(np.max(np.abs(direction)))

        improved = False
        if maxd > 1e-30:
            step = 10.0 / (float(n) * maxd)
            best_new = None
            best_new_sc = best_sc

            for frac in (0.02, 0.05, 0.10, 0.30):
                nxt = best_p - (frac * step) * direction
                nxt = clip_normalize(nxt)
                if nxt is None:
                    continue
                sc = fast_score(nxt)
                if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_new_sc:
                    best_new_sc = sc
                    best_new = nxt

            if best_new is not None and best_new_sc < best_sc:
                best_p = best_new
                best_sc = best_new_sc
                improved = True
                fail = 0
            else:
                fail += 1
                # Small Gaussian move on the simplex, accepted if almost neutral.
                if fail % 17 == 0:
                    noise = 0.03 * rng.normal(0.0, 1.0, n)
                    nxt = clip_normalize(best_p + noise * best_p)
                    if nxt is not None:
                        sc = fast_score(nxt)
                        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < best_sc * 1.005:
                            best_p = nxt
                            if sc < best_sc:
                                best_sc = sc
                            improved = True
                            fail = 0

        if fail > 120:
            # Random structured restart near the central shape.
            base = central_sequence(n, normalize=True)
            t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n - 1))
            alpha = float(rng.normal(0.0, 0.75))
            nxt = normalize_simplex(base * np.exp(alpha * t))
            if nxt is not None:
                best_p = nxt
            fail = 0

        if not improved and it > 0 and it % 5000 == 0:
            # Occasionally adaptively enlarge the active set if stuck.
            k_active = int(min(256, k_active * 2))

        it += 1

    return best_p, best_sc


def make_variants(base, rng, count=28):
    """Generate a batch of plausible transformations of a base sequence."""
    n = base.size
    t = np.arange(n, dtype=np.float64) / float(max(1, n - 1))
    out = [base.copy()]

    # Geometric slopes.
    for a in (-1.5, -1.0, -0.5, -0.25, 0.25, 0.5, 1.0, 1.5):
        out.append(base * np.exp(a * t))

    # Mild linear variations.
    for a in (-0.5, 0.5):
        out.append(base * (1.0 + 0.5 * a * t))

    # Low-frequency sinusoidal shapes.
    for k in (1, 2, 3):
        out.append(base * np.exp(0.25 * np.sin(2.0 * math.pi * k * t)))

    # Smooth random multiplicative noise.
    n_rand = max(1, count - len(out))
    for _ in range(n_rand):
        sigma = rng.normal(0.0, 0.12, n)
        out.append(base * np.exp(0.25 * sigma))

    normalized = []
    for p in out:
        pn = normalize_simplex(p)
        if pn is not None:
            normalized.append(pn)
    return normalized


def x_to_p(x, n):
    """Recover simplex vector from free log-ratios x_i = log(p_i/p_0)."""
    theta = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    theta[0] = 0.0
    theta[1:] = x
    mx = float(theta.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        mx = 0.0
    theta -= mx
    e = np.exp(theta)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return None
    return e / s


def _seq_obj_grad(x):
    """
    Objective + gradient for scipy.L-BFGS-B on free log-ratios.
    Objective: T * logsumexp(q/T) shifted by max(q), where q = p*p.
    """
    n = int(_OPT_N)
    T = float(_OPT_T)
    N = int(_OPT_NFFT)

    if n <= 1:
        return 0.0, np.zeros(0, dtype=np.float64)

    theta = np.empty(n, dtype=np.float64)
    theta[0] = 0.0
    theta[1:] = x
    mx = float(theta.max())
    if not np.isfinite(mx):
        mx = 0.0
    theta -= mx
    e = np.exp(theta)
    s = float(e.sum())
    if not np.isfinite(s) or s <= 0.0:
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    p = e / s
    P = np.fft.rfft(p, N)
    q = np.fft.irfft(P * P, N)
    q = q[:2 * n - 1]
    m = float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m):
        return 1e100, np.zeros(n - 1, dtype=np.float64)

    if T <= 1e-15:
        # Fallback: use active top coefficients.
        k = min(16, q.size)
        active = top_k_indices(q, k)
        w = np.zeros_like(q)
        w[active] = 1.0 / float(k)
        val = m
    else:
        diff = q - m
        diff = np.minimum(diff, 0.0)
        u = diff / T
        u = np.clip(u, -745.0, 0.0)
        eu = np.exp(u)
        se = float(np.sum(eu))
        if not np.isfinite(se) or se <= 0.0:
            w = np.full_like(q, 1.0 / float(q.size))
            val = m
        else:
            w = eu / se
            val = m + T * (math.log(se) - math.log(float(q.size)))

    W = np.fft.rfft(w, N)
    corr = np.fft.irfft(np.conj(W) * P, N)
    corr = corr[:n]
    v = 2.0 * corr
    if not np.all(np.isfinite(v)):
        v = np.nan_to_num(v, nan=0.0, posinf=0.0, neginf=0.0)

    mean_v = float(np.dot(p, v))
    grad_full = p * (v - mean_v)
    grad = grad_full[1:]
    grad[~np.isfinite(grad)] = 0.0

    if not np.isfinite(val):
        val = m
    return float(val), grad


def run_scipy_refine(p, maxiter=120):
    """
    Run scipy L-BFGS-B from a normalized sequence p, with several smoothing
    temperatures. Returns the best normalized sequence found.
    """
    p = normalize_simplex(p)
    if p is None:
        return None, float("inf")

    best_p = p.copy()
    best_sc = fast_score(best_p)

    q = fft_convolve_full(best_p)
    m = float(np.max(q)) if q.size else 0.0
    if not np.isfinite(m) or m <= 0.0:
        return best_p, best_sc

    if scipy_minimize is None:
        return best_p, best_sc

    global _OPT_N, _OPT_T, _OPT_NFFT
    n = best_p.size
    _OPT_N = n
    _OPT_NFFT = next_pow2(3 * n - 2)

    pp = best_p.copy()
    pp[pp <= 0.0] = 1e-300
    x0 = np.log(pp[1:]) - np.log(pp[0])
    x0 = np.clip(np.nan_to_num(x0, nan=0.0, posinf=30.0, neginf=-30.0), -30.0, 30.0)

    for frac in (0.50, 0.20, 0.08):
        _OPT_T = max(1e-12, frac * m)
        try:
            res = scipy_minimize(
                _seq_obj_grad,
                x0.copy(),
                jac=True,
                method="L-BFGS-B",
                disp=False,
                options={
                    "maxiter": int(maxiter),
                    "ftol": 1e-12,
                    "gtol": 1e-5,
                    "maxls": 20,
                },
            )
            xr = np.asarray(res.x, dtype=np.float64)
            xr[~np.isfinite(xr)] = 0.0
            xr = np.clip(xr, -50.0, 50.0)
            p2 = x_to_p(xr, n)
            if p2 is not None:
                sc2 = fast_score(p2)
                if np.isfinite(sc2) and sc2 < best_sc:
                    best_sc = sc2
                    best_p = p2
                    x0 = xr
        except Exception:
            pass

    return best_p, best_sc


def propose_candidate(seed=42, budget_s=1800, **kwargs):
    """
    Main search entry point.
    Returns a list of non-negative floats.
    """
    rng = np.random.default_rng(int(seed) if seed is not None else 42)
    start_time = time.monomonic() if hasattr(time, "monotonic") else time.monotonic()
    start_time = time.monotonic()
    deadline = start_time + max(0.0, float(budget_s) - 8.0)

    best_score = float("inf")
    best_p = None

    # 1) Central-sequence baselines for several useful lengths.
    #    8192 is a safe trade-off between score and convolution cost.
    central_lengths = (256, 512, 1024, 2048, 4096, 8192)
    for n in central_lengths:
        if time.monotonic() > deadline:
            break
        p = central_sequence(n, normalize=True)
        best_score, best_p = consider_candidate(best_score, best_p, p)

    # 2) If the provided previous sequence exists, consider it too.
    try:
        hs = np.asarray(height_sequence_1, dtype=np.float64).ravel()
        if hs.size > 0 and hs.size <= 20000:
            hs = np.clip(hs, 0.0, 1000.0)
            best_score, best_p = consider_candidate(best_score, best_p, hs)
    except Exception:
        pass

    # 3) Build a good start for refinement at a moderate length.
    opt_n = 4096 if float(budget_s) > 400 else 2048
    base = central_sequence(opt_n, normalize=True)
    var_start = base.copy()
    var_start_sc = fast_score(var_start)

    variants = make_variants(base, rng, count=32)
    for v in variants:
        if v is None:
            continue
        v = normalize_simplex(v)
        if v is None:
            continue
        sc = fast_score(v)
        if np.isfinite(sc) and sc < var_start_sc:
            var_start_sc = sc
            var_start = v
        best_score, best_p = consider_candidate(best_score, best_p, v)

    # 4) Smooth nonlinear refinement (L-BFGS-B in log-ratio coordinates).
    if scipy_minimize is not None and time.monotonic() < deadline - 20.0:
        p_scipy, sc_scipy = run_scipy_refine(var_start, maxiter=120)
        if p_scipy is not None and np.isfinite(sc_scipy):
            best_score, best_p = consider_candidate(best_score, best_p, p_scipy)
            if sc_scipy < var_start_sc:
                var_start = p_scipy
                var_start_sc = sc_scipy

    # 5) Projected-gradient / basin-hopping polish on the best moderate-length start.
    if time.monotonic() < deadline - 5.0:
        p_cg, sc_cg = custom_refine(
            var_start,
            deadline=deadline - 2.0,
            rng=rng,
            k_active=48,
            max_iter=100000,
        )
        if p_cg is not None and np.isfinite(sc_cg):
            best_score, best_p = consider_candidate(best_score, best_p, p_cg)

    # Final fallback.
    if best_p is None:
        best_p = central_sequence(1024, normalize=True)

    best_p = normalize_simplex(best_p)
    if best_p is None:
        best_p = central_sequence(1024, normalize=True)

    # Scale to a robust total mass of 100; this keeps all entries far below 1000
    # and far above the 0.01 sum guard.
    final = best_p * 100.0
    maxv = float(np.max(final))
    if np.isfinite(maxv) and maxv > 900.0:
        final *= (900.0 / maxv)
    final = np.clip(final, 0.0, 1000.0)

    return [float(x) for x in final]
```
